Q1. The tens place has \(6\), and all digits add to \(8\). Which number is it?
A. \(63\)
B. \(61\)
C. \(64\)
D. \(62\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
6+u=8\Rightarrow u=2
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 62.

\[
N=10\times 6+2=62
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 62 is 8.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(62\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=2
\]

\[
N=62
\]

Fast result: \(62\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_missing_digit_single_rule
---
Q2. If the digit sum is \(7\) and the tens digit is \(4\), what number is formed?
A. \(44\)
B. \(42\)
C. \(45\)
D. \(43\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
4+u=7\Rightarrow u=3
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 43.

\[
N=10\times 4+3=43
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 43 is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(43\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=3
\]

\[
N=43
\]

Fast result: \(43\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_missing_digit_multi_rule
---
Q3. The number \(363x1\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(2\)
B. \(0\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=1
\]

\[
1-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
1-x=0
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
1-1=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
---
Q4. Between \(179\) and \(297\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(16\)
B. \(14\)
C. \(17\)
D. \(15\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 179 is 184.

\[
8\times 23=184
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 296 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 297.

\[
297=8\times 37+1
\]

\[
297-1=296
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{296-184}{8}+1=14+1=15
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 15.
Hence, there are 15 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(15\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{297}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{178}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
37-22=15
\]

Fast result: \(15\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_divisibility_multi_condition
---
Q5. Between \(156\) and \(250\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(13\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(14\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 156 is 160.

\[
8\times 20=160
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 248 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 250.

\[
250=8\times 31+2
\]

\[
250-2=248
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{248-160}{8}+1=11+1=12
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 12.
Hence, there are 12 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{250}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{155}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
31-19=12
\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q7. Two cyclic events repeat every \(84\) and \(204\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?
A. \(1571\)
B. \(1714\)
C. \(1285\)
D. \(1428\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
84=2^{2}\times 3\times 7
\]

\[
204=2^{2}\times 3\times 17
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(84,204)=1428
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1428.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1428\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(84,204)=\frac{84\times 204}{\operatorname{HCF}(84,204)}=1428
\]

Fast result: \(1428\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_divisibility_lcm_bridge

Q8. After prime factorising \(12\), what is the number of positive divisors?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
12=2^{2}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Apply the (+1) rule on exponents

Each exponent contributes one more choice than its value.

\[
d(12)=(2+1)\times (1+1)=6
\]

Answer Verification
The number of positive divisors is 6.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Multiply (exponent + 1) across all prime powers.

\[
(2+1)\times (1+1)
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_hidden_divisor_deduction
---

Q14. After prime factorising \(12\), what is the number of odd divisors?
A. \(3\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
12=2^{2}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Count odd divisors only

Ignore the power of 2; odd divisors come from odd primes only.

\[
(1+1)
\]

\[
O=2
\]

Answer Verification
The number of odd divisors is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Drop the factor 2 and multiply (e+1) for remaining primes.

\[
O(n)=(1+1)=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q15. After prime factorising \(36\), what is the sum of all positive divisors?
A. \(92\)
B. \(90\)
C. \(93\)
D. \(91\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
36=2^{2}\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(36)=91
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 91.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(91\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{3}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{3}-1}{3-1}
\]

Fast result: \(91\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_sum_of_divisors
---
Q16. After prime factorising \(24\), what is the product of all positive divisors?
A. \(364954\)
B. \(414720\)
C. \(497664\)
D. \(331776\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
24=2^{3}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(24)=(3+1)\times (1+1)=8
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 24 d=24^{d(24)/2}=24^{8/2}=331776
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 331776.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(331776\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
24^{8/2}=331776
\]

Fast result: \(331776\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_product_of_divisors
---
Q17. Bells ring at intervals of \(63\) and \(63\) minutes. After how many minutes will both ring together again?
A. \(62\)
B. \(65\)
C. \(63\)
D. \(64\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
63=3^{2}\times 7
\]

\[
63=3^{2}\times 7
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(63,63)=63
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 63.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(63\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(63,63)=\frac{63\times 63}{\operatorname{HCF}(63,63)}=63
\]

Fast result: \(63\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q25. When \(3^{35}\) is divided by \(13\), what remainder is left?
A. \(8\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(35-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 9.

\[
3^{35}\equiv 9\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(35-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

\[
3^{35}\equiv 9\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_remainder_after_division
---

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

Hence, remainder = 1.

\]

Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

\[
p=(46-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

A. \(10\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 6+5=65
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 5+6=56
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |6-5|=9\times 1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |6-5|=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[

\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(14\).

Shortcut

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{5}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{60}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(14\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q52. Between \(224\) and \(328\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(10\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 224 is 228.

\[
12\times 19=228
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 324 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 328.

\[
328=12\times 27+4
\]

\[
328-4=324
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{324-228}{12}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{328}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{223}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
27-18=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

A. \(764\)
B. \(801\)
C. \(692\)
D. \(728\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
104=2^{3}\times 13
\]

\[
56=2^{3}\times 7
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(104,56)=728
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 728.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(728\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(104,56)=\frac{104\times 56}{\operatorname{HCF}(104,56)}=728
\]

Fast result: \(728\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q88. The number \(670x2\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=15
\]

\[
S+x=15+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
15+x=18
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
15+3=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q89. For the number \(1440\), find the sum of its positive divisors?
A. \(5897\)
B. \(4423\)
C. \(4914\)
D. \(5405\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
1440=2^{5}\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(1440)=4914
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 4914.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4914\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{6}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{3}-1}{3-1}\times \frac{5^{2}-1}{5-1}
\]

Fast result: \(4914\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 5 dividing 40! is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Perfect powers are controlled by prime exponents.

\[

\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a square, every exponent must be even.

\[
2^{3}\to 2^{4}
\]

\[
5^{2}\to 5^{2}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
200\times 2=400
\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=400
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(400\).

Shortcut

Fill only the missing prime powers; do not search through squares or cubes.

\[
M=2
\]

Fast result: \(400\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q104. Between \(184\) and \(320\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(23\)
B. \(24\)
C. \(22\)
D. \(25\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 184 is 186.

\[
6\times 31=186
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 318 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 320.

\[
320=6\times 53+2
\]

\[
320-2=318
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{318-186}{6}+1=22+1=23
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 23.
Hence, there are 23 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(23\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{320}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{183}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
53-30=23
\]

Fast result: \(23\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q107. When \(7^{76}\) is divided by \(11\), what remainder is left?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(76-1)\bmod 10+1=6
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
7^{76}\equiv 4\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 6th term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(76-1)\bmod 10+1=6
\]

\[
7^{76}\equiv 4\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q120. Between \(154\) and \(253\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(10\)
B. \(13\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 154 is 162.

\[
9\times 18=162
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 252 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 253.

\[
253=9\times 28+1
\]

\[
253-1=252
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{252-162}{9}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{253}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{153}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
28-17=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(156,72)=936
\]

Final Answer

Fast result: \(936\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q122. Power \(13^{96}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(0\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 0\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 1.

\[
0
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(96-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 0.

\[
13^{96}\equiv 0\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(96-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

\[
0^{96}\equiv 0\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_remainder_after_division

\]

Q149. When \(3^{34}\) is divided by \(5\), what remainder is left?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
3\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(34-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
3^{34}\equiv 4\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(34-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

\[
3^{34}\equiv 4\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |6-9|=9\times 3=27
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 27.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(27\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |6-9|=27
\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q173. Between \(219\) and \(336\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(19\)
B. \(22\)
C. \(20\)
D. \(21\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 219 is 222.

\[
6\times 37=222
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 336 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 336.

\[
336=6\times 56+0
\]

\[
336-0=336
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{336-222}{6}+1=19+1=20
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 20.
Hence, there are 20 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(20\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{336}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{218}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
56-36=20
\]

Fast result: \(20\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q176. Power \(3^{77}\) is tested modulo \(5\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(3\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
3\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(77-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
3^{77}\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(77-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

\[
3^{77}\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q198. Between \(119\) and \(253\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(10\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 119 is 126.

\[
18\times 7=126
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 252 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 253.

\[
253=18\times 14+1
\]

\[
253-1=252
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{252-126}{18}+1=7+1=8
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 8.
Hence, there are 8 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{253}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{118}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
14-6=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q199. After prime factorising \(12\), what is the sum of all positive divisors?
A. \(29\)
B. \(27\)
C. \(30\)
D. \(28\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
12=2^{2}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(12)=28
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 28.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(28\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{3}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{2}-1}{3-1}
\]

Fast result: \(28\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

A. \(5\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 6\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 2.

\[
6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(69-1)\bmod 2+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 6.

\[
13^{69}\equiv 6\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 6.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(69-1)\bmod 2+1=1
\]

\[
6^{69}\equiv 6\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(44-1)\bmod 6+1=2
\]

\[
2^{44}\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q229. Between \(180\) and \(330\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(25\)
B. \(28\)
C. \(26\)
D. \(27\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 180 is 180.

\[
6\times 30=180
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 330 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 330.

\[
330=6\times 55+0
\]

\[
330-0=330
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{330-180}{6}+1=25+1=26
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 26.
Hence, there are 26 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(26\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{330}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{179}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
55-29=26
\]

Fast result: \(26\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q240. The number \(414x8\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(0\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 8, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 17; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
17+x=18
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
17+1=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\]

Answer Verification

Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(280\).

\]

Fast result: \(280\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 116 is 117.

\[
9\times 13=117
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 234 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 239.

\[
239=9\times 26+5
\]

\[
239-5=234
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{234-117}{9}+1=13+1=14
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 14.
Hence, there are 14 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(14\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{239}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{115}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
26-12=14
\]

Fast result: \(14\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 222 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 223.

\[
223=6\times 37+1
\]

\[

\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{222-144}{6}+1=13+1=14
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 14.
Hence, there are 14 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(14\).

\[

Fast result: \(14\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q281. For the number \(30\), find the product of its positive divisors?
A. \(1012500\)
B. \(1215000\)
C. \(810000\)
D. \(891000\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
30=2\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(30)=(1+1)\times (1+1)\times (1+1)=8
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 30 d=30^{d(30)/2}=30^{8/2}=810000
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 810000.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(810000\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
30^{8/2}=810000
\]

Fast result: \(810000\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q282. Power \(13^{57}\) is tested modulo \(5\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(3\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
3\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(57-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
13^{57}\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(57-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

\[
3^{57}\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

\]

Fast result: \(195\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q307. Between \(153\) and \(299\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 153 is 165.

\[
15\times 11=165
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 285 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 299.

\[
299=15\times 19+14
\]

\[
299-14=285
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{285-165}{15}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{299}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{152}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
19-10=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 2.

\[
3\rightarrow 0
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(27-1)\bmod 2+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
3^{27}\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(27-1)\bmod 2+1=1
\]

\[
3^{27}\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Answer: C

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 45.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(45\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |3-8|=45
\]

Fast result: \(45\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q318. The number \(9x096\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=6
\]

\[
6-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
6-x=0
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
6-6=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q334. When \(7^{54}\) is divided by \(11\), what remainder is left?
A. \(2\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(54-1)\bmod 10+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
7^{54}\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(54-1)\bmod 10+1=4
\]

\[
7^{54}\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_factorial_remainder

Q340. Between \(157\) and \(308\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(10\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 157 is 165.

\[
15\times 11=165
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 300 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 308.

\[
308=15\times 20+8
\]

\[
308-8=300
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{300-165}{15}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{308}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{156}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
20-10=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

Fast result: \(270\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q354. Number \(48\) is reversed by interchanging its digits. What absolute difference is obtained?
A. \(38\)
B. \(36\)
C. \(37\)
D. \(35\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 4+8=48
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 8+4=84
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |4-8|=9\times 4=36
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 36.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(36\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |4-8|=36
\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=7
\]

\[
7-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
7-x=0
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
7-7=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

D. \(10\)

\[

\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(36-1)\bmod 10+1=6
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 9.

\[
2^{36}\equiv 9\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 6th term of the cycle is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

\[
p=(36-1)\bmod 10+1=6
\]

\[

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q369. Between \(220\) and \(327\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 220 is 225.

\[
15\times 15=225
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 315 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 327.

\[
327=15\times 21+12
\]

\[
327-12=315
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{315-225}{15}+1=6+1=7
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 7.
Hence, there are 7 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{327}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{219}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
21-14=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

D. \(10077696\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[

\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(36)=(2+1)\times (2+1)=9
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 36 d=36^{d(36)/2}=36^{9/2}=10077696
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 10077696.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(10077696\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
36^{9/2}=10077696
\]

Fast result: \(10077696\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q392. Power \(11^{50}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(4\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 11\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
11\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(50-1)\bmod 12+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
11^{50}\equiv 4\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(50-1)\bmod 12+1=2
\]

\[
11^{50}\equiv 4\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q401. The number \(736x0\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 0, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 16; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
16+x=18
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
16+2=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 72.

\[
N=10\times 7+2=72
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 72 is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(72\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=2
\]

\[
N=72
\]

Fast result: \(72\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q417. Between \(145\) and \(231\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(12\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 145 is 152.

\[
8\times 19=152
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 224 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 231.

\[
231=8\times 28+7
\]

\[
231-7=224
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{224-152}{8}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{231}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{144}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
28-18=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q418. Cycle position \(58\) is needed for powers of \(19\) modulo \(17\). What is the remainder?
A. \(4\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
19\equiv 2\pmod{17}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 8.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 16\rightarrow 15\rightarrow 13\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(58-1)\bmod 8+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
19^{58}\equiv 4\pmod{17}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(58-1)\bmod 8+1=2
\]

\[
2^{58}\equiv 4\pmod{17}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

Q444. The number \(1x870\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=16
\]

\[
S+x=16+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
16+x=18
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
16+2=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q445. For exponent \(34\), what ending digit does \(2^{34}\) have?
A. \(5\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{10}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 6
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(34-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
2^{34}\equiv 4\pmod{10}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(34-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

\[
2^{34}\equiv 4\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q447. Number \(87\) is reversed by interchanging its digits. What absolute difference is obtained?
A. \(11\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 8+7=87
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 7+8=78
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |8-7|=9\times 1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |8-7|=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q454. The number \(892x4\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 23.

\[
23+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+4=44
\]

\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
23+x=27
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
23+4=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+4\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q460. Between \(139\) and \(272\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(12\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(13\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 139 is 144.

\[
12\times 12=144
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 264 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 272.

\[
272=12\times 22+8
\]

\[
272-8=264
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{264-144}{12}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{272}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{138}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
22-11=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q477. The number \(4x216\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 6, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 13; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
13+x=18
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
13+5=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |3-7|=9\times 4=36
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 36.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(36\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q484. The number \(298x6\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 25.

\[
25+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+6=56
\]

\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
25+x=30
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
25+5=30
\]
\[
30\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+6\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Answer: A

\[
11\equiv 4\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(88-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
11^{88}\equiv 4\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(88-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

\[
4^{88}\equiv 4\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q514. The number \(892x6\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 25.

\[
25+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+6=56
\]

\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
25+x=30
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
25+5=30
\]
\[
30\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+6\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q524. The number \(74x29\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(2\)
B. \(0\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=10
\]

\[
10+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
10+x=11
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
10+1=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q526. For the number \(12\), find the product of its positive divisors?
A. \(2074\)
B. \(1555\)
C. \(1728\)
D. \(1901\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
12=2^{2}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(12)=(2+1)\times (1+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 12 d=12^{d(12)/2}=12^{6/2}=1728
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 1728.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1728\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
12^{6/2}=1728
\]

Fast result: \(1728\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q527. Power \(11^{44}\) is tested modulo \(7\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 4\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(44-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[
11^{44}\equiv 2\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(44-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

\[
4^{44}\equiv 2\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q551. The number \(292x6\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 19.

\[
19+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+6=56
\]

\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
19+x=24
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
19+5=24
\]
\[
24\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+6\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q557. Between \(214\) and \(330\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(12\)
B. \(15\)
C. \(13\)
D. \(14\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 214 is 216.

\[
9\times 24=216
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 324 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 330.

\[
330=9\times 36+6
\]

\[
330-6=324
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{324-216}{9}+1=12+1=13
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 13.
Hence, there are 13 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(13\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{330}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{213}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
36-23=13
\]

Fast result: \(13\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

A. \(11\)
B. \(9\)

D. \(8\)
Answer: B

Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 109 is 120.

\[

\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 216 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 225.

\[
225=12\times 18+9
\]

\[
225-9=216
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{216-120}{12}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{225}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{108}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[

\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
11^{98}\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

\[
p=(98-1)\bmod 6+1=2
\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q581. The number \(476x2\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 19.

\[
19+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+2=52
\]

\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
19+x=24
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
19+5=24
\]
\[
24\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+2\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

C. \(152\)
D. \(160\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
32=2^{5}
\]

\[
80=2^{4}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(32,80)=160
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 160.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(160\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(32,80)=\frac{32\times 80}{\operatorname{HCF}(32,80)}=160
\]

Fast result: \(160\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

Step 2: Carry the result through the next condition

This makes the final candidate satisfy the full chain, not only the first clue.

\[
A=4
\]

Answer Verification
The reconstructed value is 4.
\[
N=4
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

\[
Z=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q611. The number \(598x6\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 28.

\[
28+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+6=56
\]

\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
28+x=33
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
28+5=33
\]
\[
33\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+6\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 624.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(624\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(624\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q613. When \(13^{94}\) is divided by \(9\), what remainder is left?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
4\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(94-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
13^{94}\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(94-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

\[
4^{94}\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q624. Between \(138\) and \(290\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(26\)
B. \(27\)
C. \(25\)
D. \(28\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 138 is 138.

\[
6\times 23=138
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 288 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 290.

\[
290=6\times 48+2
\]

\[
290-2=288
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{288-138}{6}+1=25+1=26
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 26.
Hence, there are 26 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(26\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{290}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{137}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
48-22=26
\]

Fast result: \(26\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q632. Between \(189\) and \(274\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 189 is 192.

\[
12\times 16=192
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 264 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 274.

\[
274=12\times 22+10
\]

\[
274-10=264
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{264-192}{12}+1=6+1=7
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 7.
Hence, there are 7 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{274}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{188}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
22-15=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[

\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
7^{58}\equiv 4\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

\[
p=(58-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

\[
2^{58}\equiv 4\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q638. The number \(76x49\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=6
\]

\[
6+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
6+x=11
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
6+5=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\]

\[
x=4
\]

\[

\]
\[

Final Answer

\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q662. The number \(210x8\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 11.

\[
11+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+8=48
\]

\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
11+x=15
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
11+4=15
\]
\[
15\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+8\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q668. Between \(124\) and \(289\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(10\)
B. \(13\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 124 is 135.

\[
15\times 9=135
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 285 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 289.

\[
289=15\times 19+4
\]

\[
289-4=285
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{285-135}{15}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{289}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{123}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
19-8=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

\[
2^{3}\to 2^{3}
\]

\[
3^{2}\to 3^{3}
\]

\[
5^{1}\to 5^{3}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
360\times 75=27000
\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=27000
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(27000\).

Shortcut

\[
M=75
\]

Fast result: \(27000\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q682. The number \(685x3\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=6
\]

\[
6-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
6-x=0
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
6-6=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[

Answer Verification
The LCM is 105.

Final Answer

\]

Fast result: \(105\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q692. The number \(758x0\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 20.

\[
20+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+0=40
\]

\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
20+x=24
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
20+4=24
\]
\[
24\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+0\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q703. Between \(225\) and \(305\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(13\)
B. \(14\)
C. \(12\)
D. \(15\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 225 is 228.

\[
6\times 38=228
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 300 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 305.

\[
305=6\times 50+5
\]

\[
305-5=300
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{300-228}{6}+1=12+1=13
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 13.
Hence, there are 13 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(13\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{305}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{224}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
50-37=13
\]

Fast result: \(13\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[

Final Answer

\[

Q712. The number \(79x14\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 4, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 21; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
21+x=27
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
21+6=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q713. When \(3^{53}\) is divided by \(7\), what remainder is left?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
3\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(53-1)\bmod 6+1=5
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 5.

\[
3^{53}\equiv 5\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 5th term of the cycle is 5.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(53-1)\bmod 6+1=5
\]

\[
3^{53}\equiv 5\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q715. With tens \(5\) and ones \(6\), a two-digit number is written. What difference appears after reversing?
A. \(9\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 5+6=56
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 6+5=65
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |5-6|=9\times 1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |5-6|=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q719. The number \(936x8\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 26.

\[
26+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+8=48
\]

\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
26+x=30
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
26+4=30
\]
\[
30\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+8\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility

Q725. Between \(114\) and \(236\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(14\)
B. \(15\)
C. \(13\)
D. \(16\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 114 is 117.

\[
9\times 13=117
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 234 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 236.

\[
236=9\times 26+2
\]

\[
236-2=234
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{234-117}{9}+1=13+1=14
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 14.
Hence, there are 14 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(14\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{236}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{113}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
26-12=14
\]

Fast result: \(14\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q736. The number \(4x214\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 4, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 11; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
11+x=18
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
11+7=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility

Q737. When \(11^{92}\) is divided by \(7\), what remainder is left?
A. \(1\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 4\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(92-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[
11^{92}\equiv 2\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(92-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

\[
4^{92}\equiv 2\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

\[

\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{50}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{50}{16}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{50}{32}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
25+12+6+3+1=47
\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 2 dividing 50! is 47.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(47\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{50}{2}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{50}{4}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{50}{8}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{50}{16}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{50}{32}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(47\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q742. Between \(187\) and \(317\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 187 is 195.

\[
15\times 13=195
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 315 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 317.

\[
317=15\times 21+2
\]

\[
317-2=315
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{315-195}{15}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{317}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{186}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
21-12=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q767. Between \(129\) and \(262\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(15\)
B. \(16\)
C. \(14\)
D. \(17\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 129 is 135.

\[
9\times 15=135
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 261 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 262.

\[
262=9\times 29+1
\]

\[
262-1=261
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{261-135}{9}+1=14+1=15
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 15.
Hence, there are 15 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(15\).

Shortcut

\[

\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(15\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q768. After prime factorising \(18\), what is the product of all positive divisors?
A. \(6415\)
B. \(6998\)
C. \(5249\)
D. \(5832\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
18=2\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(18)=(1+1)\times (2+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 18 d=18^{d(18)/2}=18^{6/2}=5832
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 5832.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(5832\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
18^{6/2}=5832
\]

Fast result: \(5832\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[

\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 8.

\[
2^{39}\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 8.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(39-1)\bmod 6+1=3
\]

\[
2^{39}\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q789. The number \(554x2\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 16.

\[
16+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+2=52
\]

\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
16+x=21
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
16+5=21
\]
\[
21\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+2\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility

Q790. Power \(11^{69}\) is tested modulo \(7\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 4\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(69-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
11^{69}\equiv 1\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(69-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

\[
4^{69}\equiv 1\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).
