A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=17
\]

\[
17-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
17-x=11
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
17-6=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

\]

\[
x=8
\]

\[

\]
\[

Final Answer

\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q816. The number \(75x70\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 0, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 19; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
19+x=27
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
19+8=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility

Q817. When \(2^{38}\) is divided by \(13\), what remainder is left?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(38-1)\bmod 12+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
2^{38}\equiv 4\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(38-1)\bmod 12+1=2
\]

\[
2^{38}\equiv 4\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[
N=900
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(900\).

A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=17
\]

\[
S+x=17+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
17+x=18
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
17+1=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q829. Between \(151\) and \(324\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(19\)
B. \(22\)
C. \(20\)
D. \(21\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 151 is 153.

\[
9\times 17=153
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 324 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 324.

\[
324=9\times 36+0
\]

\[
324-0=324
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{324-153}{9}+1=19+1=20
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 20.
Hence, there are 20 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(20\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{324}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{150}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
36-16=20
\]

Fast result: \(20\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q833. The number \(44x29\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=7
\]

\[
7+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
7+x=11
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
7+4=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q840. The number \(610x8\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 8, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 15; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
15+x=18
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
15+3=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
---
Q841. When \(2^{30}\) is divided by \(7\), what remainder is left?
A. \(0\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(30-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
2^{30}\equiv 1\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(30-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

\[
2^{30}\equiv 1\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

Step 2: Carry the result through the next condition

This makes the final candidate satisfy the full chain, not only the first clue.

\[
A=11
\]

Answer Verification

\[
N=11
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

\[
Z=11

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q847. The number \(810x8\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 17.

\[
17+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+8=48
\]

\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
17+x=21
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
17+4=21
\]
\[
21\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+8\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

A. \(29\)
B. \(27\)
C. \(28\)
D. \(26\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 8+5=85
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 5+8=58
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |8-5|=9\times 3=27
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 27.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(27\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |8-5|=27
\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q856. Between \(189\) and \(368\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(24\)
B. \(22\)
C. \(25\)
D. \(23\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 189 is 192.

\[
8\times 24=192
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 368 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 368.

\[
368=8\times 46+0
\]

\[
368-0=368
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{368-192}{8}+1=22+1=23
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 23.
Hence, there are 23 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(23\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{368}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{188}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
46-23=23
\]

Fast result: \(23\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

A. \(5\)

C. \(4\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=22
\]

\[
S+x=22+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
22+x=27
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
22+5=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q867. Between \(136\) and \(240\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(18\)
B. \(19\)
C. \(17\)
D. \(20\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 136 is 138.

\[
6\times 23=138
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 240 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 240.

\[
240=6\times 40+0
\]

\[
240-0=240
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{240-138}{6}+1=17+1=18
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 18.
Hence, there are 18 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{240}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{135}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
40-22=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q878. The number \(258x0\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=15
\]

\[
S+x=15+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
15+x=18
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
15+3=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

Q893. What is the least perfect square that is divisible by \(252\)?
A. \(1588\)
B. \(1764\)
C. \(1940\)
D. \(2117\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Factorize the given number

Perfect powers are controlled by prime exponents.

\[
252=2^{2}\times 3^{2}\times 7
\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a square, every exponent must be even.

\[
2^{2}\to 2^{2}
\]

\[
3^{2}\to 3^{2}
\]

\[
7^{1}\to 7^{2}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
252\times 7=1764
\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=1764
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(1764\).

Shortcut

Fill only the missing prime powers; do not search through squares or cubes.

\[
M=7
\]

Fast result: \(1764\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q895. Between \(133\) and \(223\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(13\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(12\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 133 is 136.

\[
8\times 17=136
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 216 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 223.

\[
223=8\times 27+7
\]

\[
223-7=216
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{216-136}{8}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{223}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{132}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
27-16=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[

\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

\[

\]

\[
4^{66}\equiv 1\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification

\[

\]
\[

\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

\[

\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q916. The number \(976x4\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 26.

\[
26+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+4=44
\]

\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
26+x=30
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
26+4=30
\]
\[
30\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+4\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q919. How many trailing zeroes are there in \(50!\)?
A. \(13\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(14\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Count powers of 5 in n!

Trailing zeroes come from factor pairs 2×5; count fives.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{50}{5}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{50}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
10+2=12
\]

Answer Verification
Trailing zeroes in 50! = 12.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Sum only ⌊n/5⌋ + ⌊n/25⌋ + …

\[

Q923. The number \(83x50\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=16
\]

\[
S+x=16+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
16+x=18
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
16+2=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q924. Two cyclic events repeat every \(48\) and \(120\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?
A. \(252\)
B. \(264\)
C. \(228\)
D. \(240\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
48=2^{4}\times 3
\]

\[
120=2^{3}\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(48,120)=240
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 240.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(240\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(48,120)=\frac{48\times 120}{\operatorname{HCF}(48,120)}=240
\]

Fast result: \(240\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q925. Power \(3^{43}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(3\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(43-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
3^{43}\equiv 3\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(43-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

\[
3^{43}\equiv 3\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q929. Between \(193\) and \(281\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 193 is 195.

\[
15\times 13=195
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 270 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 281.

\[
281=15\times 18+11
\]

\[
281-11=270
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{270-195}{15}+1=5+1=6
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 6.
Hence, there are 6 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{281}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{192}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
18-12=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=8
\]

\[
8-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
8-x=0
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
8-8=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q947. The number \(8x870\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?

B. \(4\)
C. \(5\)

Answer: B

Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[

\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 0, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[

\[

\]

\[

\[

\[

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(4\).

\[

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q948. For exponent \(44\), what ending digit does \(12^{44}\) have?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
12\equiv 2\pmod{10}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 6
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(44-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 6.

\[
12^{44}\equiv 6\pmod{10}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 6.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(44-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

\[
2^{44}\equiv 6\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q951. The number \(854x0\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 17.

\[
17+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+0=40
\]

\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
17+x=21
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
17+4=21
\]
\[
21\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+0\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q961. Between \(133\) and \(259\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(13\)
B. \(16\)
C. \(14\)
D. \(15\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 133 is 135.

\[
9\times 15=135
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 252 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 259.

\[
259=9\times 28+7
\]

\[
259-7=252
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{252-135}{9}+1=13+1=14
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 14.
Hence, there are 14 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(14\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{259}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{132}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
28-14=14
\]

Fast result: \(14\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[
x=7
\]

Final Answer

\[

A. \(16\)
B. \(17\)
C. \(15\)
D. \(18\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 113 is 114.

\[
6\times 19=114
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 204 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 206.

\[
206=6\times 34+2
\]

\[
206-2=204
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{204-114}{6}+1=15+1=16
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 16.
Hence, there are 16 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{206}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{112}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
34-18=16
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

\[
7^{79}\equiv 8\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_factorial_remainder
---
Q1000. For \(19^{116}\), use the cycle modulo \(17\). What remainder is left?
A. \(16\)
B. \(17\)
C. \(15\)
D. \(18\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
19\equiv 2\pmod{17}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 8.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 16\rightarrow 15\rightarrow 13\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(116-1)\bmod 8+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 16.

\[
19^{116}\equiv 16\pmod{17}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 16.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(116-1)\bmod 8+1=4
\]

\[
2^{116}\equiv 16\pmod{17}
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_multi_cluster_reasoning
